I don’t want to give the wrong answer. Maybe it’s more muted? Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite over the past three years?\n\nPatient: Um… appetite? I don’t think I’ve noticed anything major, honestly. It’s kind of hard to say over that long a stretch.\n\nI mean, I eat… maybe some days are different than others, but I haven’t really kept track or thought of it as a problem. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep patterns over the past three years?\n\nPatient: Sleep… I’m not sure. I mean, I sleep, so I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nOver the past few years I haven’t really kept track of it. Some nights are better than others, I guess, but I don’t know if I’d call it a pattern or a “change.” What would count as a change, exactly?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel tired or lacking in energy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess I do. I don’t want to make it sound like I can’t function, because I still get things done, but I’m tired a lot.\n\nLike by midmorning, my body can feel like I’ve already put in a full day. Even if I sleep in on a Sunday, I still kind of drag through basic stuff—loading tools, looking over job plans, making dinner. It feels heavier than it should, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or worthless about things?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know. I mean, I feel bad about things sometimes, sure, but I’m not sure I’d say “worthless.” That sounds pretty strong.\n\nI guess I can be hard on myself if I don’t handle something the way I think I should, but I don’t want to overstate it. Is that what you’re asking, like whether it’s a constant thing?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think about death or dying?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know how to answer that the right way.\n\nI mean, I know everybody thinks about it sometimes, right? Like when you hear about somebody passing or you’re tired and your mind kind of goes there for a second. I don’t want you thinking I’m trying to be dramatic or anything.\n\nAre you asking if I’m, like… planning something, or just whether the thought crosses my mind?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says.   },

    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Completely free of it? Um… no, I don’t think so. I wish I could say yes.

There are days where I’m distracted enough, like if I’m out with the crew or around my kids, and maybe I can laugh or act normal for a while. But underneath it, most days I wake up already feeling like there’s this gray weight sitting on me. I guess I still do some of the same things, like I’ll show up or go along with stuff, but whether I’m really enjoying it the way I used to… I don’t know.\n\nI don’t want to overstate it. It’s more like I’m present, you know? I can smile and be there. But I haven’t really stopped to think if it feels the same as before. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say I’ve lost interest exactly… I mean, I still do things if people ask me to, and I try not to be a drag about it.\n\nBut I guess it doesn’t really pull me in the same way. Like, I’ll go through with it, but sometimes it feels more like I’m doing what I’m supposed to do than because I’m looking forward to it. Is that kind of what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to experience pleasure from activities or things that you used to find enjoyable?\n\nPatient: I guess… I’m not totally sure. I don’t know that I’ve paid close attention to it like that.\n\nThere are still moments where something is nice, or I can tell myself it should be nice, but I’m not sure if I’m really feeling pleasure from it the way you mean. I don’t want to give the wrong answer. Maybe it’s more muted? Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite over the past three years?\n\nPatient: Um… appetite? I don’t think I’ve noticed anything major, honestly. It’s kind of hard to say over that long a stretch.\n\nI mean, I eat… maybe some days are different than others, but I haven’t really kept track or thought of it as a problem. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep patterns over the past three years?\n\nPatient: Sleep… I’m not sure. I mean, I sleep, so I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nOver the past few years I haven’t really kept track of it. Some nights are better than others, I guess, but I don’t know if I’d call it a pattern or a “change.” What would count as a change, exactly?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel tired or lacking in energy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess I do. I don’t want to make it sound like I can’t function, because I still get things done, but I’m tired a lot.\n\nLike by midmorning, my body can feel like I’ve already put in a full day. Even if I sleep in on a Sunday, I still kind of drag through basic stuff—loading tools, looking over job plans, making dinner. It feels heavier than it should, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or worthless about things?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know. I mean, I feel bad about things sometimes, sure, but I’m not sure I’d say “worthless.” That sounds pretty strong.\n\nI guess I can be hard on myself if I don’t handle something the way I think I should, but I don’t want to overstate it. Is that what you’re asking, like whether it’s a constant thing?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think about death or dying?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know how to answer that the right way.\n\nI mean, I know everybody thinks about it sometimes, right? Like when you hear about somebody passing or you’re tired and your mind kind of goes there for a second. I don’t want you thinking I’m trying to be dramatic or anything.\n\nAre you asking if I’m, like… planning something, or just whether the thought crosses my mind?\n\nDoctor: Do you have recurring thoughts about death or dying that aren't just passing thoughts?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a way where it keeps coming back and takes over, if that’s what you mean.\n\nSometimes the thought pops up, like I said, but I wouldn’t say I’m sitting around thinking about death over and over. I don’t want to give you the wrong impression. It’s more like… it crosses my mind, and then I move on.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods of feeling significantly better over the past three years?\n\nPatient: Not really, no… I mean, I’ve had a good day here and there, I guess, but not a stretch where I felt really back to myself.\n\nIf I’m being honest, it’s been around three years now… a little over, maybe three years and a few months. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but I haven’t had a real period where I felt significantly better since then.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days over the past three years where you felt completely free of this low mood?\n\nPatient: Completely free of it? Um… no, I don’t think so. I wish I could say yes.\n\nThere are days where I’m distracted enough, like if I’m out with the crew or around my kids, and maybe I can laugh or act normal for a while. But underneath it, most days I wake up already feeling like there’s this gray weight sitting on me. Even on the better days, it’s still kind of there… that low, hollow feeling, like I’m just going through the motions.\n\nI don’t know if that answers it right, but no, I can’t really think of a day where it was just completely gone.\n\nDoctor: Do you think this low mood has been present every day for the past three years?\n\nPatient: Every day… I mean, pretty much, yeah. I don’t want to overstate it, but most days I wake up and it’s already there, like this gray weight sitting on me.\n\nEven if I’m with the crew or around my kids and I can get through things, maybe even seem okay for a bit, I don’t really shake it. I don’t want to make it sound like I can’t function, because I still get things done, but I’m tired a lot.\n\nLike by midmorning, my body can feel like I’ve already put in a full day. Even if I sleep in on a Sunday, I still kind of drag through basic stuff—loading tools, looking over job plans, making dinner. I mean, I sleep, so I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nOver the past few years I haven’t really kept track of it. I don’t want to make it sound like I can’t function, because I still get things done, but I’m tired a lot.\n\nLike by midmorning, my body can feel like I’ve already put in a full day. Even if I sleep in on a Sunday, I still kind of drag through basic stuff—loading tools, looking over job plans, making dinner. I don’t want to give you the wrong impression. I wake up and it’s like there’s this gray weight on me already, before anything even happens.\n\nI still go to work and I’m around the crew, and I’m around my kids, so I don’t want to make it sound like I’m not trying… but it’s like I’m just going through the motions. There’s this kind of hollow feeling I can’t really shake. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s been a while. I don’t want to make it sound too dramatic, but probably about three years now—maybe three years and a few months.\n\nI think it started when the groundskeeping contract changed and our schedule got longer. The days just kind of took over my life after that, and I guess I never really bounced back from it. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like before you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not sure how to answer that exactly. I guess I still do some of the same things, like I’ll show up or go along with stuff, but whether I’m really enjoying it the way I used to… I don’t know.\n\nI don’t want to overstate it. It’s more like I’m present, you know? I can smile and be there. But I haven’t really stopped to think if it feels the same as before. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say I’ve lost interest exactly… I mean, I still do things if people ask me to, and I try not to be a drag about it.\n\nBut I guess it doesn’t really pull me in the same way. Like, I’ll go through with it, but sometimes it feels more like I’m doing what I’m supposed to do than because I’m looking forward to it. Is that kind of what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to experience pleasure from activities or things that you used to find enjoyable?\n\nPatient: I guess… I’m not totally sure. I don’t know that I’ve paid close attention to it like that.\n\nThere are still moments where something is nice, or I can tell myself it should be nice, but I’m not sure if I’m really feeling pleasure from it the way you mean. I don’t want to give the wrong answer. Maybe it’s more muted? Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite over the past three years?\n\nPatient: Um… appetite? I don’t think I’ve noticed anything major, honestly. It’s kind of hard to say over that long a stretch.\n\nI mean, I eat… maybe some days are different than others, but I haven’t really kept track or thought of it as a problem. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep patterns over the past three years?\n\nPatient: Sleep… I’m not sure. I mean, I sleep, so I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nOver the past few years I haven’t really kept track of it. Some nights are better than others, I guess, but I don’t know if I’d call it a pattern or a “change.” What would count as a change, exactly?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel tired or lacking in energy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess I do. I don’t want to make it sound like I can’t function, because I still get things done, but I’m tired a lot.\n\nLike by midmorning, my body can feel like I’ve already put in a full day. Even if I sleep in on a Sunday, I still kind of drag through basic stuff—loading tools, looking over job plans, making dinner. It feels heavier than it should, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or worthless about things?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know. I mean, I feel bad about things sometimes, sure, but I’m not sure I’d say “worthless.” That sounds pretty strong.\n\nI guess I can be hard on myself if I don’t handle something the way I think I should, but I don’t want to overstate it. Is that what you’re asking, like whether it’s a constant thing?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think about death or dying?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know how to answer that the right way.\n\nI mean, I know everybody thinks about it sometimes, right? Like when you hear about somebody passing or you’re tired and your mind kind of goes there for a second. I don’t want you thinking I’m trying to be dramatic or anything.\n\nAre you asking if I’m, like… planning something, or just whether the thought crosses my mind?\n\nDoctor: Do you have recurring thoughts about death or dying that aren't just passing thoughts?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a way where it keeps coming back and takes over, if that’s what you mean.\n\nSometimes the thought pops up, like I said, but I wouldn’t say I’m sitting around thinking about death over and over. I don’t want to give you the wrong impression. It’s more like… it crosses my mind, and then I move on.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods of feeling significantly better over the past three years?\n\nPatient: Not really, no… I mean, I’ve had a good day here and there, I guess, but not a stretch where I felt really back to myself.\n\nIf I’m being honest, it’s been around three years now… a little over, maybe three years and a few months. It kind of started after the groundskeeping contract changed and our schedule got longer. Those longer days just sort of took over my life. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but I haven’t had a real period where I felt significantly better since then.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days over the past three years where you felt completely free of this low mood?\n\nPatient: Completely free of it? Um… no, I don’t think so. I wish I could say yes.\n\nThere are days where I’m distracted enough, like if I’m out with the crew or around my kids, and maybe I can laugh or act normal for a while. But underneath it, most days I wake up already feeling like there’s this gray weight sitting on me. Even on the better days, it’s still kind of there… that low, hollow feeling, like I’m just going through the motions.\n\nI don’t know if that answers it right, but no, I can’t really think of a day where it was just completely gone.\n\nDoctor: Do you think this low mood has been present every day for the past three years?\n\nPatient: Every day… I mean, pretty much, yeah. I don’t want to overstate it, but most days I wake up and it’s already there, like this gray weight sitting on me.\n\nEven if I’m with the crew or around my kids and I can get through things, maybe even seem okay for a bit, I don’t really shake it. It’s still this low, hollow feeling underneath, like I’m just going through the motions. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you had two or more years where you felt down or depressed for most of the day, more days than not?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, I think that’s fair to say. It’s been about three years and three months now, more or less.\n\nIt really started after that groundskeeping contract changed and our schedule got longer. Those longer days kind of took over my life, and since then I’ve been down most of the day, more days than not. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but… yeah, it’s been more than two years for sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience pleasure from things that most people would find enjoyable?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean… I don’t think I’m completely unable to enjoy things. Sometimes I can still have a decent moment, or laugh at something, especially if other people are around.\n\nBut if you mean whether things feel as good as they probably should… I guess maybe not always. It’s hard to tell. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks over the past three years where you felt completely back to normal and didn't experience any of these low mood symptoms?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I wish I could say there was a stretch like that, but it’s been going on pretty steadily for about three years and three months.\n\nSince that groundskeeping contract changed and the longer days kind of took over my life, I don’t think I’ve had two weeks where I felt completely back to normal. Maybe a decent day here and there, but not a real break like that. Does that sound like what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks where you felt sad or depressed nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes. I mean, I don’t want to overstate it, but there have definitely been more than two weeks where it’s been nearly every day.\n\nMost mornings I wake up and it’s like this gray weight is already sitting on me. Even when I’m out with the crew or around my kids, I’m there, but I feel kind of hollow… like I’m just going through the motions. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods, even brief, where your low mood significantly improved or lifted over the past three years?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, maybe there were little moments where it wasn’t quite as heavy, but I can’t think of a real period where it lifted in any meaningful way.\n\nIt’s been pretty constant for about three years and three months now, since that groundskeeping contract changed our schedule and the longer days kind of took over my life. I don’t want to sound dramatic, but… I don’t think I’ve had a true break from it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks where you felt depressed nearly every day and also experienced at least four other symptoms like changes in appetite, sleep, energy, or interest in activities?\n\nPatient: I think so… at least with the energy part. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but a lot of days by midmorning my body feels like I’ve already put in a full day.\n\nEven if I sleep in on a Sunday, I still feel like I’m dragging through basic stuff… loading tools, checking the job plans, even making dinner. It’s embarrassing to admit, because I know everybody gets tired, but this feels like more than normal tired. Is that the kind of symptom you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
